#+TITLE: Project Work Ledger
#+STARTUP: overview
#+TODO: TODO ACTIVE BLOCKED REVIEW | DONE ABANDONED
#+TYP_TODO: PENDING FAILING SUPERSEDED | HOLD ASSUMED_PASS
#+TAGS: feature defect maintenance migration legacy
#+OPTIONS: toc:2 num:nil

This is the project's canonical work and requirements index. Each fact has one
authoritative representation:

- *Current lifecycle state* :: The TODO keyword on the work-item headline.
- *Classification* :: One or more tags on that headline.
- *Stable metadata* :: The work item's property drawer.
- *Current definition* :: The linked =spec.org= file.
- *Evidence* :: The linked =audits.org= and =validation.org= files.
- *History* :: Git history and explicitly linked archived sources.

Do not repeat lifecycle state in a property, section, specification, or evidence
file. Work items remain under one heading when their state changes. Use one
monotonically increasing project-wide work number and never reuse an identifier,
including after abandonment, deletion, retirement, or archival.

* Ledger vocabulary
:PROPERTIES:
:VISIBILITY: folded
:END:

The file directives declare the permitted TODO states and standard tags. This
section defines their meanings.

** Lifecycle states

- =TODO= :: Defined but not started.
- =ACTIVE= :: Delivery is underway.
- =BLOCKED= :: Delivery cannot continue because of a named external dependency.
- =REVIEW= :: Human review, validation, or disposition is required.
- =DONE= :: Delivered, verified, and closed.
- =ABANDONED= :: Deliberately not delivered. Its identifiers remain reserved.

** Legacy requirement states

- =PENDING= :: The requirement or some required evidence remains unverified.
- =FAILING= :: Current evidence shows that the requirement does not hold.
- =SUPERSEDED= :: A named later requirement replaces or retires it.
- =HOLD= :: The requirement remains current and its recorded evidence passes.
- =ASSUMED_PASS= :: Migration inferred delivery under a recorded heuristic; it
  is not a contemporaneous test result.

** Standard tags

- =feature= :: New or changed product behaviour.
- =defect= :: Incorrect existing behaviour.
- =maintenance= :: Internal upkeep without intended product-behaviour change.
- =migration= :: Historical or framework migration work.
- =legacy= :: Material governed by an earlier delivery process. Apply it to the
  parent headline so the whole subtree inherits the classification.

Tags are non-exclusive classifications. Add a project-specific tag only when it
provides a durable cross-cutting category that the standard tags cannot express.

** Work-item structure

#+begin_example
** TODO WNNN - Concise descriptor :feature:
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: w-nnn
:PRIORITY: P1
:SOURCE: operator
:CREATED: YYYY-MM-DD
:END:

- *Specification:* [[file:../specs/NNN-concise-descriptor/spec.org][WNNN - Concise descriptor]]
#+end_example

Use =OWNER= and =BRANCH= properties only when concurrent work needs explicit
coordination. Use =MIGRATION_DISPOSITION= only to preserve an immutable migration
classification; it is provenance, not current state.

* Work items

:PROPERTIES:

:PRIORITY: P1
:SOURCE: operator

* Legacy Acceptance Criteria (SDLC v1) :legacy:
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: legacy-acceptance-criteria
:VISIBILITY: folded
:SOURCE_DATE: 2026-09-03
:END:

** Overview

This is the canonical specification of delivered system behaviour. It retains the v1 product lineage and records the current v2 product in the same ledger.

V1 established local upscaling through the command-line tool and native Mac application. V2 is a substantial UX change and architectural extension: the Mac application combines explicit cloud image generation and transformation with the local Core ML pipeline in one native workspace, while the command-line tool remains a local-upscaling interface.

The 2026-09-03 migration retired GitHub issues as the acceptance-criteria store. Legacy criteria are preserved below with their identifiers, provenance, test evidence and supersession lineage. Defined but undelivered scope is recorded in =ticket-migration.org= and is not accepted here.


** Legacy ledger authority at migration

This file is the sole authoritative record of requirements established under
the legacy ticket-led process, including current and superseded requirements,
provenance, and test traceability.

The migration index and archived tickets retain disposition, provenance, and
rationale only. Ticket bodies and comments are not current requirement or AC
authorities.


** Legacy status vocabulary at migration

- *HOLDING:* The requirement remains current and its recorded evidence passes.
- *PENDING:* The requirement or some required evidence remains unverified.
- *FAILING:* Current evidence shows the requirement does not hold.
- *SUPERSEDED:* A named later requirement replaces or retires it.
- *ASSUMED PASS:* Migration inferred delivery under a named heuristic; this is
  not a contemporaneous test result.

Per-test status follows the same words, plus *NOTED* for a source line that
cross-references another criterion's tests rather than asserting a result of its
own.

** Initial v2 architecture and superseded UX lineage

Tickets #70 through #78 introduced v2 as a substantial UX change and architectural extension of v1. Their initial four-workspace proposal was superseded by the single-workspace design in =IMPLEMENTATION_GUIDE_v2.md=. The criteria below preserve implemented architectural lineage and identify the UX clauses that the later design replaced.

*** HOLD AC70.1 - Given the v2 foundations, the =Superscale= CLI remains a local-upscaling executable with no dependency on GUI-only generation modules.

**** Requirement

Given the v2 foundations, the =Superscale= CLI remains a local-upscaling executable with no dependency on GUI-only generation modules.

**** Provenance

- *Introduced by:* [[archive/migrated-tickets/70.md][#70]] (legacy ticket)
- *Shipped or migrated:* 2026-09-03

**** Status qualification

the ticket's four-workspace specification was superseded by =docs/IMPLEMENTATION_GUIDE_v2.md=; implemented code and criterion lineage are retained.
**** Tests

- *PASS* =RT-70.1=: Package dependency tests prove the CLI target depends on =SuperscaleKit= and excludes =FalGenerationKit= and =SuperscaleUXCore=.
- *PASS* =RT-70.2=: CLI help and a small fixture upscale path remain available after the v2 foundation lands.

**** Requirement

**** Provenance

the ticket's four-workspace specification was superseded by =docs/IMPLEMENTATION_GUIDE_v2.md=; implemented code and criterion lineage are retained.

*** HOLD AC81.8 - A stage accepts only an asset reference obtained from the graph, so an arbitrary file location cannot be submitted for processing.

- *Shipped or migrated:* 2026-08-23

**** Tests

- *PASS* =RT-81.27=: a stage rejects an asset reference the graph does not hold
- *PASS* =RT-81.28=: the session-to-stage path yields the asset the session produced rather than any upscale of it
- *PASS* =RT-81.29=: the display path still resolves the finished image, so presentation is unaffected

**** Notes

- the exclusion of bare file locations is a property of the stage signature and carries no test. A test cannot assert that an overload is absent, because code calling a function that does not exist would not compile. The evidence is that the application target builds after the change while the seven correct uses of the display accessor are untouched.

** Stages and the scale control

*** HOLD AC82.1 - Stage progress identifies the phase of work as a value, and carries the counts that belong to it --- faces enhanced, tiles completed and total --- as numbers.

**** Requirement

Stage progress identifies the phase of work as a value, and carries the counts that belong to it --- faces enhanced, tiles completed and total --- as numbers.

**** Provenance

- *Introduced by:* #82 (closed 2026-08-23)
- *Shipped or migrated:* 2026-08-23

**** Tests

- *PASS* =RT-82.1=: each phase the local pipeline reports arrives as a distinct case, and none of them resolves to the unclassified case
- *PASS* =RT-82.2=: the number of faces enhanced is available without reading any message text
- *PASS* =RT-82.3=: tile progress arrives as completed and total counts rather than as text
- *PASS* =RT-82.26=: a progress report the mapping does not recognize still reaches the caller rather than being dropped
- *PASS* =RT-83.20=: the stage's phase mapping yields a distinct case for every phase the kit reports, with none resolving to the unclassified case
- *PASS* =RT-83.21=: a kit phase the stage does not map reaches the caller as unclassified rather than being dropped

**** Notes

- RT-82.1, RT-82.2, RT-82.3 and RT-82.26 were rewritten in #83 against =PipelineProgress=, when the kit began reporting phases as values rather than as sentences the stage parsed. The criterion is unchanged and the IDs are unchanged; only the source the mapping reads from moved. RT-83.20 and RT-83.21 were added to this criterion's coverage by the same issue.
- the mapping from the pipeline's wording to a phase is coupled to that wording until structured progress lands inside =SuperscaleKit=. The coupling lives in one adapter, and the unclassified case is what stops an unmapped message being lost meanwhile.

*** HOLD AC82.2 - Local upscaling and cloud filtering present one run-state model and one error path, so a caller handles both the same way.

**** Requirement

**** Provenance

- *Introduced by:* #82 (closed 2026-08-23)
- *Shipped or migrated:* 2026-08-23

**** Tests

*** HOLD AC82.11 - No upscale is selected when the application launches, so the first upscale of a session is one the user asked for.

**** Requirement

No upscale is selected when the application launches, so the first upscale of a session is one the user asked for.

**** Provenance

- *Introduced by:* #131 (closed 2026-08-27), backfilled onto the #82 family
- *Shipped or migrated:* 2026-08-27

**** Tests

- *PASS* =RT-131.1=: no scale is in effect on launch
- *PASS* =RT-131.2=: importing with nothing selected runs no upscale
- *PASS* =RT-131.3=: selecting a scale after import runs the upscale
- *PASS* =RT-131.7=: with nothing selected, upscaling still reads as available
- *PASS* =RT-131.4=: exactly one scale reads as in effect
- *PASS* =RT-131.5=: after clearing and reselecting, only the chosen scale reads as in effect
- *PASS* =UT-93.1=: no scale is in effect at launch, so the first upscale of a session is one the user asked for --- passed by the author in the round of 2026-08-29, recorded on master #133

**** Notes

- *two scales appearing pressed was a rendering fault, not a selection fault.* =tint()= returned a fill for =requestedNotInEffect= as well as for =inEffect=, and a tinted =.bordered= button reads as pressed --- so a ceiling reduction showed the scale running /and/ the scale asked for, both apparently selected, and the dimmer of the two read as disabled besides. The author diagnosed it as a disabled control; =ScaleReadout.isChoosable= returns true unconditionally and nothing was ever disabled. *Decision D-2* on master #120: the reduced scale gets a dashed outline instead, which keeps AC82.8's promise that the control shows what was asked for without claiming it is what runs. RT-131.4 and RT-131.5 hold the underlying state singular so a later rendering change cannot blur it again.
- *decision D-3* declined the request to disable every control above the size threshold. =ScaleReadout.swift:88= records why the controls stay pressable --- /"dimmed reads as disabled --- which would trap the user at the reduced scale until they imported a different picture"/ --- and disabling them would deliver the letter of the request while creating that trap. The appearance the request was aimed at is what D-2 fixes.
- *this reverses a documented decision rather than fixing an accident.* Guide 2.5 held that v1's immediacy --- drop a picture and it upscales at whatever scale is selected --- was the v1 experience and was kept. In use the cost was paid on the first action of every session, on an output a filter-first user is about to set aside, before they had chosen anything. Guide 2.5 amended at 3.33 *before* the code, since the behaviour being replaced was specified.
- *reactivity is untouched.* An upscale still runs whenever a scale is selected and there is something to run on. Only the starting value moved. RT-131.3 is what holds that line.
- *RT-131.2 is what makes RT-131.1 mean anything.* Clearing the selection on launch and re-selecting it on import passes RT-131.1 and changes nothing.
- the selection is deliberately not persisted, verified rather than assumed: =GenerationPreferences= carries the output folder, the two default models and the default prompt pack, and =loadDefaults= does not touch the scale. A persisted scale would make RT-131.1 pass or fail by machine, which is the shape that made RT-73.8 environment-dependent.
- *this change failed four other tests on its first run, all at one helper.* With nothing selected an import no longer upscales, so =waitForUpscaleComplete()= --- sixty-three call sites --- waited for something that would never happen. That is the change being real: sixty-three tests were relying on the application starting work nobody had asked for. The helper now asks for a scale when none is in effect, and carries a warning that a test meaning /"no upscale should happen"/ must not call it, because it would cause the very thing being checked.

*** HOLD AC82.9 - The directory beneath which the graph allocates an output location exists at the moment of allocation, independently of any other component having been constructed.

**** Requirement

The directory beneath which the graph allocates an output location exists at the moment of allocation, independently of any other component having been constructed.

**** Provenance

- *Introduced by:* #115 (closed 2026-08-26), backfilled onto the #82 stage family
- *Shipped or migrated:* 2026-08-26

**** Tests

- *PASS* =RT-115.1=: allocating a raise creates the output directory, absent before and present after
- *PASS* =RT-115.2=: the same for an upscale allocation
- *PASS* =RT-115.3=: a stage writes to a freshly allocated location with no directory prepared by any other component
- *PASS* =RT-115.4=: an output directory already holding assets is not disturbed by allocation
- *PASS* =RT-115.5=: with the output directory absent, a raise completes through the application and its result reaches the canvas
- *PASS* =RT-115.8=: allocation succeeds where the directory already exists, so the guarantee is not satisfied by throwing unconditionally

**** Notes

- *backfilled, not pre-existing.* AC82.5 constrains where a stage writes and is silent on whether the allocation is usable. =AssetGraph= minted paths beneath an =outputDirectory= it never created, and worked only because =GenerationCoordinator= created the same directory as a side effect on the ordinary launch path. The UI-test launch replaces that coordinator, the side effect disappeared, and six of the 101 GUI tests failed with /"The folder =raised-<uuid>.png= doesn't exist."/
- *RT-115.5 is the only one of them that would have caught this*, and it is at GUI level for that reason. =make test= reported 533 executed and 0 failures throughout the entire broken period, because a package test constructs the graph with a directory it made itself.
- creation happens in the allocation methods rather than in =init=. =AssetGraph= is public and its initializer is not throwing, so creating a directory there would have been a public API change and a mandatory architecture stop.

*** HOLD AC82.10 - Where the output directory cannot be brought into existence, allocation fails at the point of allocation, naming the directory and the reason, rather than yielding a location that fails when a stage writes to it.

**** Requirement

Where the output directory cannot be brought into existence, allocation fails at the point of allocation, naming the directory and the reason, rather than yielding a location that fails when a stage writes to it.

**** Provenance

- *Introduced by:* #115 (closed 2026-08-26), backfilled onto the #82 stage family
- *Shipped or migrated:* 2026-08-26

**** Tests

- *PASS* =RT-115.6=: where the directory cannot be created, allocation throws rather than returning a location
- *PASS* =RT-115.7=: the failure names the directory and carries the underlying reason, so two distinct impossibilities are distinguishable

**** Notes

- AC82.9 alone admits the defect one layer along, in an implementation that guarantees existence on the happy path and stays silent when creation fails. RT-115.7 compares the two failures with the directory removed from each, so a message that names the directory and drops the reason fails.

*** HOLD AC83.7 - An upscale's output never exceeds the supported area, whatever asked for it: a scale is reduced to the largest that fits, a custom target is reduced proportionally, and the reduction is reported while the scale selection continues to show what was asked for. A picture that cannot fit at any scale is left as it is, with the reason given. The minimum long edge required for filtering is unaffected by that ceiling.

**** Requirement

An upscale's output never exceeds the supported area, whatever asked for it: a scale is reduced to the largest that fits, a custom target is reduced proportionally, and the reduction is reported while the scale selection continues to show what was asked for. A picture that cannot fit at any scale is left as it is, with the reason given. The minimum long edge required for filtering is unaffected by that ceiling.

**** Provenance

- *Introduced by:* #91 (closed 2026-08-25), backfilled against the #83 family
- *Shipped or migrated:* 2026-08-25

**** Tests

- *PASS* =RT-91.1=: an output above the ceiling is reduced to the largest scale that fits
- *PASS* =RT-91.2=: the decision reports what was asked for and what is used, and the selection is unchanged
- *PASS* =RT-91.3=: an output that fits is produced unreduced
- *PASS* =RT-91.4=: the ceiling is area, so a wide short picture and a tall narrow one of equal area are treated alike
- *PASS* =RT-91.5=: the reported case, a 2000-pixel-wide picture at 4x, yields a sizing that fits
- *PASS* =RT-91.6=: a small picture is still raised toward the filterable minimum, unblocked by the ceiling
- *PASS* =RT-91.7=: a picture that fits at no scale is left alone, with the reason given
- *PASS* =RT-91.8=: a custom target above the ceiling is reduced proportionally, preserving its aspect
- *PASS* =RT-91.9=: the ceiling binds the output produced rather than the request typed
- *PASS* =RT-91.10=: the coordinator asks its processor for an output within the ceiling
- *PASS* =RT-101.1=: with a reduction in force, the status bar reports it where a user reads it
- *PASS* =RT-101.2=: the report names the scale used and the scale requested, and they differ --- asserted within RT-101.1's test, as the suite does elsewhere
- *PASS* =RT-101.4=: with no reduction and no raise, no notice is shown at all
- *PASS* =UT-91.1=: the message explaining a reduced upscale is clear and unobtrusive --- passed by the author, 2026-08-30

**** Notes

- *#91 proved the decision, not the sentence.* Its ten tests are all package-level over =UpscaleCeiling= and assert which scale was chosen, what size resulted, and that a reduction occurred. None of them asserts that the user is ever /told/. #101 added the three regression tests above at the surface the user actually reads, so the last link --- decision to displayed sentence --- is covered rather than assumed.
- *backfilled, not pre-existing.* #91 first cited AC83.7 as a legacy criterion on #83. It did not exist: #83 carries AC83.1 to AC83.6, and the resolution caps were only ever prose in =IMPLEMENTATION_GUIDE_v2.md= 2.5 and 3.8 plus a residual-risk note. The bound was acknowledged and never specified, which is how an 8000-pixel output came to sit between a warning at 4096 and a refusal at 8192 --- permitted, and fatal. Backfilled under the second path in =ISSUES.md= §"Bug-fix issues reference existing ACs".

*** HOLD AC84.3 - No more than two pipelines are held, and the least recently used is released first.

**** Requirement

No more than two pipelines are held, and the least recently used is released first.

**** Provenance

- *Shipped or migrated:* 2026-08-23

- *PASS* =RT-84.9=: using a held pipeline makes it the most recently used, so a later release takes the other

**** Notes

- the bound is measured through reloads rather than through a published count. The second slot exists for the face-enhancement toggle, which alternates between two keys holding the same underlying model; one slot would reload on every press.

*** HOLD AC84.4 - Runs through the cache do not overlap, a run's progress reaches only its own observer, and no observer outlives its run.

**** Requirement

Runs through the cache do not overlap, a run's progress reaches only its own observer, and no observer outlives its run.

**** Provenance

- *Introduced by:* #84 (closed 2026-08-23)
- *Shipped or migrated:* 2026-08-23

**** Tests

- *PASS* =RT-84.10=: a second run does not begin until the first has returned
- *PASS* =RT-84.11=: progress reported during one run reaches that run's observer and no other
- *PASS* =RT-84.12=: a failure in one run leaves a later run unaffected
- *PASS* =RT-84.17=: a pipeline returned to the cache holds no observer from the run that used it
- *PASS* =RT-84.18=: two runs started concurrently for settings nothing is held for load the model once

**** Notes

- non-overlap and single-loading are properties of two signatures being synchronous --- the lent body and the loader. Swift actors are reentrant, so an =async= form of either would break these while the tests still passed unless the tests contrived a suspension. RT-84.10 and RT-84.18 are what make the breakage loud.

*** HOLD AC84.6 - An upscale performed by the application obtains its pipeline from the cache rather than constructing one.

**** Requirement

An upscale performed by the application obtains its pipeline from the cache rather than constructing one.

**** Provenance

- *Introduced by:* #84 (closed 2026-08-23)
- *Shipped or migrated:* 2026-08-23

**** Tests

- *PASS* =RT-84.15=: two successive upscales through the processor call the cache's loader exactly once
- *PASS* =RT-84.16=: the processor's second upscale produces the same result as its first

**** Notes

- these two need the x4plus model and skip without it. =Pipeline= is a concrete class, so a counting loader must still return a real one, and a criterion about the application's path cannot be met below that path. RT-84.15 counts /exactly/ one because a processor that bypasses the cache calls the loader zero times, which an "at most once" assertion would accept.

** The filter catalogue

*** HOLD AC74.1 - The GUI can load prompt packs with stable identifiers, display names, categories, prompt bodies, and model compatibility metadata.

**** Requirement

The GUI can load prompt packs with stable identifiers, display names, categories, prompt bodies, and model compatibility metadata.

**** Provenance

- *Introduced by:* #74 (closed, pre-cutover)
- *Shipped or migrated:* 2026-08-23, cited by #85

**** Tests

- *PASS* =RT-74.1=: valid bundled resources load
- *PASS* =RT-74.2=: identifier stability, declared name, declared category, and default FAL compatibility

**** Notes

- the mechanism changed in #85 and the criterion did not. Names and categories are read from each filter's frontmatter rather than split out of its filename, and RT-74.2 is rewritten against that while keeping its identifier. The model compatibility clause survives because =compatibleModelIDs= was never file metadata: the loader supplies the one MVP model, as it always did.
- *UT-74.1 is re-offered by #138 (2026-08-30).* It passed on 86 names; the corpus is now 108 and twenty-two more names go in front of the same judgement. RT-74.1's count moved with it. See AC138.1.

*** HOLD AC138.1 - The bundled corpus holds the twenty-two named additions alongside the original filters, each loadable, each with a well-formed header and a non-empty body.

**** Requirement

The bundled corpus holds the twenty-two named additions alongside the original filters, each loadable, each with a well-formed header and a non-empty body.

**** Provenance

- *Introduced by:* #138 (closed 2026-08-30)
- *Shipped or migrated:* 2026-08-30

**** Tests

- *PASS* =RT-138.1=: the bundled corpus contains 108 filters
- *PASS* =RT-138.2=: each of the twenty-two named identifiers is present and loadable
- *PASS* =RT-138.3=: every bundled file carries a well-formed header with id, name, category and requiresInput
- *PASS* =RT-138.4=: each new file's identifier matches its filename
- *PASS* =RT-138.6=: every bundled prompt has a non-empty body
- *PASS* =RT-74.1=: valid bundled resources load, count updated 86 to 108
- *PASS* =UT-74.1= re-offered: all 108 names read as names rather than filenames --- passed by the author, 2026-08-30

**** Notes

- transformed by one rule rather than hand-authored --- =id= from the filename, =category= from its second segment, =name= from the rest, =requiresInput= true. Twenty-two hand-written headers is twenty-two chances to differ from each other.
- bodies are copied *verbatim*. They are the author's words and the thing being paid for at the provider, so no reflowing and no tidying.
- *RT-138.3 carries a removal rather than an assertion, and it is worth reading before anyone tries again.* Two attempts were made to assert mechanically that a name "reads as a name rather than a filename". The first rejected any hyphen and failed on /Post-Vaporwave Muted/; the second compared the name against the identifier's tail and failed on /Solarpunk Civic/, which lowercases back to its own filename *because that is what the convention requires*. The property is not machine-checkable. What remains asserted is capitalization, which is. The judgement itself is UT-74.1's, which is where it was all along.
- neither the Settings count nor the filter panel's count is written down. =SettingsView= reads =packs.count= and =FilterPanel= derives its own, so 86 became 108 without either being touched.

*** HOLD AC138.2 - The Narrative and Institutional categories exist in the corpus and are non-empty, and the filter list groups by them alongside the categories already there.

**** Requirement

The Narrative and Institutional categories exist in the corpus and are non-empty, and the filter list groups by them alongside the categories already there.

**** Provenance

- *Introduced by:* #138 (closed 2026-08-30)
- *Shipped or migrated:* 2026-08-30

**** Tests

- *PASS* =RT-138.5=: the Narrative and Institutional categories are present and non-empty in the catalogue
- *PASS* =RT-138.7=: both are offered as chips in the filter list, and choosing Narrative narrows to its eight
- *PASS* =UT-74.1= re-offered: the grouping is judged with the names --- passed by the author, 2026-08-30

**** Notes

- *the ticket estimated Narrative at 11 of the 22 and it is 8.* The final split is Narrative 8, Institutional 4, Media 4, Material 3, and one each into Design, Print and Zeitgeist. The estimate was made by reading filenames before the transformation ran; the count above is from the headers as written. Recorded rather than quietly corrected because the ticket's reasoning about the primary surface was sized against the wrong number.
- *RT-138.7 closes a gap in this criterion's own test audit.* The criterion says the filter list /groups by/ the new categories, and the only test cited for it asserted they exist in the /catalogue/, which is not the list. It happens that =FilterPanel.categories= derives its chips from the loaded filters, so it passes without an application change --- but the criterion was untested either way, and a later change that hardcoded the chip list would have satisfied everything #138 shipped with.
- *growing the corpus broke two GUI tests the audit never looked at.* RT-87.10 and RT-87.36 hardcoded 86. The count is asserted in three places across the suite and derived in none; the test audit examined =PromptPackTests= and stopped there. Corrected to 108.
- adding categories changes how the /existing/ filters are found, not only the new ones. Guide 2.3 calls the filter list the primary surface, and its category list in guide 2.2 had also been missing =photo= before this ticket --- corrected there at guide 3.37.

*** HOLD AC74.2 - The prompt-pack loader rejects invalid or incomplete resources with actionable diagnostics and no secret leakage.

- *Shipped or migrated:* 2026-08-23, extended by #85

- *PASS* =RT-74.3=: an unsupported model reference is rejected

- *PASS* =RT-85.20=: a corpus with two filters declaring the same identifier is reported, naming it
- *PASS* =RT-85.21=: the bundled corpus contains no duplicate identifier

**** Notes

- duplicate rejection is not new. What changed in #85 is why duplicates are possible at all --- they were prevented by the filesystem, one identifier per filename, and frontmatter removes that guarantee at the moment 86 files are hand-edited. =pack(id:)= uses =first(where:)=, so a duplicate would make one filter permanently unreachable with no error and no way to notice but by counting.

- *PASS* =RT-85.1=: a filter's name and category are those its frontmatter declares
- *PASS* =RT-85.2=: a filter whose frontmatter disagrees with its filename presents what the frontmatter says
- *PASS* =RT-85.3=: every filter in the bundled corpus loads with a name and category

*** HOLD AC85.2 - Loading a corpus containing a file that cannot supply valid metadata fails, naming the file and the reason, and yields no filters.

**** Requirement

Loading a corpus containing a file that cannot supply valid metadata fails, naming the file and the reason, and yields no filters.

**** Provenance

- *Introduced by:* #85 (closed 2026-08-23)
- *Shipped or migrated:* 2026-08-23

**** Tests

- *PASS* =RT-85.4=: a file with no frontmatter is reported, naming the file
- *PASS* =RT-85.5=: a file whose frontmatter is malformed, or carries a field of the wrong type, is reported, naming the file
- *PASS* =RT-85.6=: a file whose frontmatter omits a required field is reported, naming the field
- *PASS* =RT-85.7=: a file whose body is empty after its frontmatter is reported
- *PASS* =RT-85.26=: a file whose frontmatter carries a required field as an empty or whitespace-only value is reported, naming the field

**** Notes

- the failure is of the corpus rather than of the file. A catalogue quietly short by one is not something anybody counts, whereas a build that will not start is noticed at once. RT-85.26 is the case a decoder cannot catch: ="name": ""= decodes into a =String= perfectly well and ships a blank row in the list. Its assertions match the /quoted/ field name, because "invalid" contains "id".

*** HOLD AC85.3 - The text a filter sends is its body verbatim, with the frontmatter no part of it, and no filter in the corpus carries its filename or a heading standing in for one.

**** Requirement

The text a filter sends is its body verbatim, with the frontmatter no part of it, and no filter in the corpus carries its filename or a heading standing in for one.

**** Provenance

- *Introduced by:* #85 (closed 2026-08-23)
- *Shipped or migrated:* 2026-08-23

**** Tests

- *PASS* =RT-85.8=: a loaded filter's text contains no frontmatter delimiter
- *PASS* =RT-85.9=: no bundled filter's text begins with a markdown heading
- *PASS* =RT-85.10=: no filter's text contains its resource name
- *PASS* =RT-85.11=: the three filters that carried a filename heading load without it
- *PASS* =RT-85.24=: a filter whose body contains a horizontal rule loads with that body intact
- *PASS* =RT-85.32=: a filter whose body begins with a markdown heading loads with that heading intact

**** Notes

- this closes D4, where three of 86 filters sent their own filename to the provider as prompt text. RT-85.32 is what makes the corpus the only place the fix can live: a loader that discarded a leading =#= line would satisfy RT-85.9 to RT-85.11 with the three files untouched, and would silently truncate the first filter that legitimately opened with a heading. RT-85.24 covers the other direction, =---= being a horizontal rule as well as a delimiter.

*** HOLD AC85.4 - Choosing a filter yields its text for editing and sends nothing, replacing whatever the field held.

**** Requirement

Choosing a filter yields its text for editing and sends nothing, replacing whatever the field held.

**** Provenance

- *Introduced by:* #85 (closed 2026-08-23)
- *Shipped or migrated:* 2026-08-23

**** Tests

- *PASS* =RT-85.12=: choosing a filter yields that filter's text
- *PASS* =RT-85.13=: choosing a filter issues no request
- *PASS* =RT-85.14=: choosing a second filter replaces the text rather than appending to it
- *PASS* =RT-85.25=: choosing the filter already chosen yields its text afresh, discarding any edit
- *PASS* =RT-85.29=: choosing a filter replaces hand-written text entered with no filter chosen

**** Notes

- RT-85.13 is the point of the two-step flow --- selecting must be free, so a user can read eighty-six filters at no cost. RT-85.25 is how a user reverts an edit they have decided against; without it the only way back to the built-in wording is to choose something else and return, which works by accident rather than by design.

- *PASS* =RT-85.16=: applying an edited filter sends the edited text
- *PASS* =RT-85.17=: an edit is discarded when another filter is chosen, so the built-in filter is unchanged

**** Notes

- supersedes AC74.3. The composition it specified --- pack body joined to user text at send time --- is what the two-step flow removes; the half that survives, /without mutating the bundled resource/, is this criterion's third condition, and RT-74.5 and RT-74.6 are rewritten into RT-85.17 rather than left calling a =PromptComposer= that no longer exists.

*** HOLD AC85.8 - What is applied is the text as it stands, whether it came from a filter or was written by hand, and applying with no text to send is refused.

**** Requirement

What is applied is the text as it stands, whether it came from a filter or was written by hand, and applying with no text to send is refused.

**** Provenance

- *Introduced by:* #85 (closed 2026-08-23)
- *Shipped or migrated:* 2026-08-23

**** Tests

- *PASS* =RT-85.22=: applying with text entered and no filter chosen sends that text
- *PASS* =RT-85.23=: applying with neither text nor a filter chosen issues no request, and the apply action is unavailable
- *PASS* =RT-85.27=: applying with a filter chosen and its text cleared issues no request, and the apply action is unavailable
- *PASS* =RT-85.28=: applying with text consisting only of whitespace issues no request, and the apply action is unavailable

**** Notes

- supersedes AC74.4's half about editing controls. The refusal belongs to the text rather than to the pair of (text, filter): a filter sitting chosen beside an empty box is still nothing to send, and an empty prompt reaches a paid edit endpoint and returns something arbitrary. It lives in the model that builds the request, so the disabled button and the send path cannot disagree.

*** HOLD AC85.9 - A corpus that fails to load leaves the application with no filters and with the reason available, and leaves local upscaling unaffected.

A corpus that fails to load leaves the application with no filters and with the reason available, and leaves local upscaling unaffected.

- *Shipped or migrated:* 2026-08-23

**** Tests

- *PASS* =RT-85.31=: after a corpus fails to load, the upscale action remains available and its settings are unaffected

**** Notes

- section 2.8 of =IMPLEMENTATION_GUIDE_v2.md= rules that when filters are unavailable, local upscaling works fully. A corpus that will not load is that condition reached by another route, and the route should not change what the user gets. The handling lives in =GenerationSettingsState= rather than in the app's entry point so that it is the same code the tests drive.

*** PENDING AC146.1 - A credential row keeps its arrangement whatever is typed into its field, and the rows below it do not move.

**** Requirement

A credential row keeps its arrangement whatever is typed into its field, and the rows below it do not move.

**** Provenance

- *Introduced by:* #146 (closed 2026-08-31)
- *Shipped or migrated:* 2026-08-31

**** Tests

- *PASS* =RT-146.1, RT-146.2, RT-146.3=: every control in the credentials section keeps its position while a long key is typed
- *PASS* =RT-146.4=: both credential rows use the same arrangement as each other
- *PENDING* =UT-73.2= re-offered: type a long key into each field and judge that nothing jumps

**** Notes

- *two causes, one level apart.* The row was a =LabeledContent=, which on macOS puts the label beside the content until the content's ideal width will not fit and then reflows it above --- and a =TextField='s ideal width follows its text, so a long FAL key changed the arrangement mid-keystroke. Underneath that, =minWidth= alone left the field itself growing with the key and pushing the save, remove and status controls along the row.
- *#110 already fought this and won half of it.* Its note on the status slot records that swapping a spinner for a badge /"made =LabeledContent= renegotiate the form's label column --- and the whole form moved under the user's typing."/ It pinned the slot and left the field free to do the same thing by another route, which is why RT-146.1-3 measures *every* control in the section rather than trusting one proxy.
- label-above is what a populated row already looked like, so nothing moves when a key is pasted, and it gives the field the full width --- which suits a credential sixty characters long.
- diagnosed from the author's two screenshots, which show the same row in both arrangements.
- *UT-73.2 is re-offered.* He passed it on 2026-08-30, before trying a long key in the admin field.

*** HOLD AC73.6 - Every interactive control in Settings is individually addressable by assistive technology, rather than being absorbed into the row that contains it.

**** Requirement

Every interactive control in Settings is individually addressable by assistive technology, rather than being absorbed into the row that contains it.

- *PASS* =RT-88.1=: the account refresh control is reachable in the accessibility tree
- *PASS* =RT-88.2=: the account summary is reachable in the accessibility tree
- *PASS* =RT-88.3=: each control AC73.5 names is reachable by its own identifier

**** Notes

- backfilled rather than stretched from AC73.5. A sighted user could see the refresh control; it was drawn, on screen, and worked with a mouse. What it was not was addressable, so VoiceOver could not reach it. An accessibility identifier on an =HStack= makes SwiftUI treat the stack as one element and absorb its children. Isolated by comparison with the pricing row directly above, which carries no container identifier and whose button was always reachable. RT-88.3 states the rule rather than the two instances, because the same mistake elsewhere in the panel would leave RT-88.1 and RT-88.2 passing.

*** HOLD AC95.2 - A credential is legible while it is being entered and after it has been saved, so a pasted key can be checked by eye.

**** Requirement

A credential is legible while it is being entered and after it has been saved, so a pasted key can be checked by eye.

**** Provenance

- *Introduced by:* #95 (closed 2026-08-25)
- *Shipped or migrated:* 2026-08-25

**** Tests

- *PASS* =RT-95.3=: a typed key is readable in the field
- *PASS* =RT-95.4=: a saved key is readable when Settings is reopened

**** Notes

- a FAL key is a bearer credential, not a password recited from memory, and masking it prevents the one check anybody performs on a pasted key. Where it is /stored/ is unchanged --- the Keychain --- and so is the rule that it travels only in a request header. RT-95.3 asserts that no secure field exists rather than that this element is a text field, so the unfixed view fails it for the right reason.

*** HOLD AC95.3 - The generation key reads as working only after the provider has accepted it, and a stored but unverified key is distinguishable from both a verified one and an absent one. The account key reads as stored or absent and carries no verification state. Checking a key costs nothing, and editing one returns it to unverified.

**** Requirement

The generation key reads as working only after the provider has accepted it, and a stored but unverified key is distinguishable from both a verified one and an absent one. The account key reads as stored or absent and carries no verification state. Checking a key costs nothing, and editing one returns it to unverified.

**** Provenance

- *Introduced by:* #95 (closed 2026-08-25)
- *Shipped or migrated:* 2026-08-25

- *PASS* =RT-95.5=: a stored, unchecked generation key reads as stored rather than as working
- *PASS* =RT-95.6=: a generation key the provider accepts reads as working
- *PASS* =RT-95.7=: a generation key the provider rejects reads as rejected, with the provider's reason
- *PASS* =RT-95.8=: an unreachable provider leaves the key as stored rather than reporting it rejected
- *PASS* =RT-95.14=: the account key row shows no verification state
- *PASS* =RT-95.16=: verification issues no generation request
- *PASS* =RT-95.17=: editing a verified key returns it to unverified until it is saved and checked again
- *PASS* =RT-109.1=: a key typed into the account row and not saved reads as not configured
- *PASS* =RT-109.2=: pressing the account row's save changes what the row reports, in the same interaction
- *PASS* =RT-109.3=: editing a saved account key returns the row to not configured until it is saved again
- *PASS* =RT-109.4=: a store that throws leaves the account row saying not configured
- *PASS* =RT-109.5=: an account key already in the Keychain reads as stored on launch, with no press
- *PASS* =RT-109.6=: removing the account key returns the row to not configured
- *PASS* =RT-109.7=: saving whitespace over a stored account key removes it and reads as not configured
- *PASS* =RT-109.9=: a saved account key can still be removed while it is being edited

**** Notes

- #109 found the account half of this criterion violated. /"The account key reads as stored or absent"/ was implemented as =!accountAdministrationKey.isEmpty= --- the *text field*, not the Keychain --- so the badge flipped to "stored" on the first keystroke and the save press had no state change left to make. Beside a generation key that answers a press with a green tick, a row that answers with nothing reads as a broken button, and it was reported as one. The state now holds the account key as it was last stored and compares the field against it.
- *the word "stored" means something slightly different in the two rows, and deliberately so.* For the generation key it means "typed but unverified", which RT-95.17 requires. For the account key it means "matches the Keychain". The generation row's press has its own answer to deliver, the provider's verdict, so its badge need not report the store; the account row's press has no other answer, so its badge must report the only thing the press changes. Recorded so nobody later tidies the two into agreement and breaks RT-95.17.
- *the remove control reads a different question from the badge.* The badge asks whether the field matches the Keychain; remove asks whether anything is in the Keychain. Left on the badge, it would have disabled itself the moment a saved key was edited, so a user correcting a typo could no longer delete the key they were correcting. RT-109.9 is the guard.
- RT-109.4, RT-109.5 and RT-109.7 are unit tests rather than GUI ones, and that is a cost paid deliberately. There is no honest way to make the Keychain refuse from XCUITest --- adding a =keychain= mode to =SUPERSCALE_UI_TEST_FAIL= would have the shipping app carry a failure injector to reach one assertion reachable in-process --- and seeding a key "already stored at launch" would mean a GUI test writing the author's own login Keychain, the wall that also retired RT-111.5.
- *RT-109.3 and RT-109.5 are the two that matter.* Flipping a flag in the save button's action satisfies RT-109.2 and fails both: returning to not-configured on an /edit/ needs the field compared against what was stored, and a flag resets on launch where a Keychain read does not.
- the badge read from whether a key was /stored/, so a typo saved and showed a green tick. RT-95.8 is the one that matters most: reporting an unreachable provider as a rejection has the user delete a working key. RT-95.16 checks the request's own properties --- a GET, no body, one call, no model name and no queue path in the URL --- because the client most obviously to hand generates images at 2c each, and verifying by generating would charge a user for checking whether they typed their key correctly.
- a verdict belongs to the key it was given, so the state holds the checked key alongside the answer and derives the badge from both. Resetting on edit would need an ordering between a keystroke and a reply, and there is none to be had.

*** HOLD AC95.6 - A credential row's controls occupy the same space whatever the row's state, so entering text, saving, checking and the result of a check change what the row says and never what it measures.

**** Requirement

A credential row's controls occupy the same space whatever the row's state, so entering text, saving, checking and the result of a check change what the row says and never what it measures.

**** Provenance

- *Introduced by:* #110 (closed 2026-08-27), backfilled onto the #95 family
- *Shipped or migrated:* 2026-08-27

**** Tests

- *PASS* =RT-110.1=: the row's frame is unchanged between empty and filled
- *PASS* =RT-110.2=: the row's frame is unchanged between the checking state and each settled state
- *PASS* =RT-110.3=: the form's other rows do not move while one row changes state

**** Notes

- *backfilled, not cited.* The originating ticket quoted AC95.1 as /"the whole scene reads cleanly"/; AC95.1 says /"each credential row names its field once, and that name is identifiable rather than merely visible"/, which is about naming. Nothing in the #95 family covered layout stability, so this is path 2 of =ISSUES.md= §"Bug-fix issues reference existing ACs".
- the mechanism was a =ProgressView= and a status badge swapped directly into the row's =HStack=. Their intrinsic widths differ, so each state change resized the stack, then the =TextField= beside it, then the form's label column through =LabeledContent=. A fixed-width slot holds both.
- *RT-110.3 is the one closest to the complaint.* /"The layout of the whole form jumps about"/ is about the rows the user is not typing into. It also catches the shortest wrong fix, which pins the field's width and leaves the trailing content free to push the row around.

*** HOLD AC95.7 - The account key row states in the scene that its key is held for later use and is not checked with the provider, so a row that never turns green is explained rather than merely quiet.

**** Requirement

The account key row states in the scene that its key is held for later use and is not checked with the provider, so a row that never turns green is explained rather than merely quiet.

**** Provenance

- *Introduced by:* #109 (closed 2026-08-27), backfilled onto the #95 family
- *Shipped or migrated:* 2026-08-27

**** Tests

- *PASS* =RT-109.8=: the account row says, in readable text, that its key is not verified

**** Notes

- *backfilled.* The state half of #109 is a straight AC95.3 violation and is recorded there. Nothing in the family covered a control explaining its own limits, so this is path 2 of =ISSUES.md= §"Bug-fix issues reference existing ACs".
- *"in the scene", not "on hover", is the whole content of the criterion.* The ticket offered the sentence as /"one short help string"/, and =.help()= in this file renders as a tooltip: invisible until hovered, invisible to anyone who does not know to hover, and unreadable from XCUITest, so RT-109.8 could not have asserted it. The author's wording is kept exactly; only the placement is changed, and that change was flagged on the ticket rather than assumed.
- the sentence sits in the section rather than as a parameter on =credentialRow=. A note belonging to one row is not a property of the shared component that draws both, and threading it through as an optional would have put a second concern into a builder #110 had just narrowed.

*** HOLD AC95.5 - Settings offers no control that operates a feature the MVP excludes. Storing a credential for later is not operating anything, so the account key row remains.

**** Requirement

Settings offers no control that operates a feature the MVP excludes. Storing a credential for later is not operating anything, so the account key row remains.

**** Provenance

- *Introduced by:* #95 (closed 2026-08-25)
- *Shipped or migrated:* 2026-08-25

**** Tests

- *PASS* =RT-95.12=: no cost-confirmation control is present
- *PASS* =RT-95.13=: no pricing or account-balance control is present
- *PASS* =RT-95.15=: the account key row is present and can be saved and cleared

**** Notes

- the line is between /storing/ a credential for later and /operating/ a paused feature. A Check Pricing button performs an operation the MVP has removed; a key field holds a value for a version that will. Asserted by identifier /and/ by the visible words, so a control renamed rather than removed does not pass.
- =costThreshold= and its stored preference go with the control, and the retired defaults key is removed on the next save rather than left for a later reader to prove is dead.

*** HOLD AC87.4 - Every category the catalogue declares narrows the filter list in one action, widens it again in one, and is reachable alongside a search that cuts across categories; a catalogue that fails to load leaves the panel stating why.

**** Requirement

Every category the catalogue declares narrows the filter list in one action, widens it again in one, and is reachable alongside a search that cuts across categories; a catalogue that fails to load leaves the panel stating why.

**** Provenance

- *Introduced by:* #87 (closed 2026-08-24)
- *Shipped or migrated:* 2026-08-24

**** Tests

- *PASS* =RT-87.8=: every category the catalogue declares is offered as a one-click narrowing
- *PASS* =RT-87.9=: choosing a category narrows the list to the filters that declare it
- *PASS* =RT-87.36=: choosing the active category again clears the narrowing
- *PASS* =RT-87.37=: search reaches across categories
- *PASS* =RT-87.10=: with no category chosen, every filter the catalogue loads is offered
- *PASS* =RT-87.31=: when the catalogue fails to load, the panel is given the reason to state

**** Notes

- reworded after the first implementation was rejected on sight. It read "every bundled filter is reachable within its category", which section headings satisfy: 86 filters in one scroll with headings between them is grouping rather than choosing. A filter bar rather than a drill-down, because the requirement is flexibility with few clicks rather than a fixed number of steps.
- *"a search that cuts across categories" was true of this criterion and false of the application for three raisings.* =visibleFilters= anded the chip and the query, so with Lighting chosen a search reached 4 filters of 108. *RT-87.37 asserted the right rule and could not fail*: it ran from a state with no chip active, so "reaches across categories" was never tested against a category. Widened by #141 to start from a chipped state, where its =linocut= assertion fails against the old code. See AC141.1.

*** PENDING AC141.1 - Entering the search field clears any active category, so a search covers the whole corpus and no chip is ever lit while not narrowing.

**** Requirement

Entering the search field clears any active category, so a search covers the whole corpus and no chip is ever lit while not narrowing.

**** Provenance

- *Introduced by:* #141 (closed 2026-08-31)
- *Shipped or migrated:* 2026-08-31

**** Tests

- *PASS* =RT-141.1=: with a category chosen, focusing the search field clears the chip
- *PASS* =RT-141.2=: a search then finds a filter from a different category
- *PASS* =RT-141.3=: the count returns to the whole corpus when the chip clears
- *PASS* =RT-141.4=: focusing with no category chosen changes nothing
- *PASS* =RT-141.6=: no chip claims to narrow while the whole corpus is listed
- *PASS* =RT-141.7=: text already typed survives the chip clearing
- *PASS* =RT-87.37= widened: search reaches across categories *from a chipped starting state*
- *PENDING* =UT-141.1=: choose a category, click the search box, and judge that searching now reaches everything

**** Notes

- *the author's third raising.* The intent had been written down and never implemented --- =FilterPanel='s own doc comment already claimed /"search cuts across categories, so nothing is hidden behind navigation."/
- *on focus, not on the first keystroke.* He wrote /"clicking in the search box"/, and clearing on a keystroke would leave one keystroke's worth of results filtered by a chip that is about to disappear.
- *the chip is cleared, not ignored.* Making the search ignore an active chip is one line and satisfies almost every test here, but leaves a chip lit while it narrows nothing --- a control that lies, which is worse than the defect. *RT-141.6 blocks that route*, and writing it required making the chip's state addressable: it had been expressed as colour and nothing else, so it reached neither VoiceOver nor a test. The same gap #95's credential badge had.

*** HOLD AC89.2 - Lock is the only action that moves the base, and it promotes the candidate at its own resolution, never the upscaled rendering of it.

**** Notes
